{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_810b3b31d2814fc997523919","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n2\t\n3\tIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\n4\tLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n5\t\n6\tPart of #35\n7\t\n8\t## Why\n9\t\n10\t`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n11\twithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n12\tEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n13\tproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\n14\tshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\n15\tcasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\n16\tthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\n17\twritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\n18\tconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n19\t\n20\tThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\n21\tto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\n22\tin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\n23\tof it.\n24\t\n25\t## What to do\n26\t\n27\tValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n28\t`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\n29\tunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\n30\tschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\n31\tproviders.\n32\t\n33\tCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\n34\tnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\n35\tresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n36\t\n37\tA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\n38\tso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n39\t`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\n40\tgeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\n41\tit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\n42\tthere is no opt-out flag.\n43\t\n44\tUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n45\t\n46\t## Acceptance criteria\n47\t\n48\t- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n49\t      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n50\t      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n51\t- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n52\t      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n53\t      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n54\t      count is `0`\n55\t- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n56\t      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n57\t      exactly once\n58\t- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n59\t      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n60\t      **not** contain the offending value\n61\t- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n62\t      that the thrown message names the tool\n63\t- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n64\t      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n65\t- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n66\t- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n67\t      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n68\t      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n69\t      round\n70\t- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n71\t      pinned by a test\n72\t- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n73\t      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n74\t      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n75\t      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n76\t      containing that field's value\n77\t- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n78\t      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n79\t      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n80\t      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n81\t- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n82\t      `devDependencies`, and\n83\t      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n84\t- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n85\t      merely present\n86\t- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n87\t      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n88\t      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n89\t      returned to the model rather than thrown\n90\t- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n91\t      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n92\t      properties pass through unmodified\n93\t- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n94\t      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n95\t      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n96\t      bump is MINOR\n97\t- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n98\t      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n99\t      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n100\t      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n101\t- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n102\t      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n103\t      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n104\t- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n105\t      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n106\t\n107\t## Tech notes\n108\t\n109\tToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n110\t`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\n111\tWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\n112\tthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n113\t`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n114\t\n115\tDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\n116\tclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\n117\tdirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\n118\tthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\n119\tevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\n120\tvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\n121\towns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\n122\tauthorization model); retry, timeout\n123\tand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\n124\twritten against this contract later.\n125\t\n126\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8d3e1359-359f-4923-a237-478972687d15","uuid":"9b9f48fb-43fc-4245-8020-4c11684d5c6e","timestamp":"2026-11-05T18:59:38.927Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/6656eaf44017/workspace/ticket.md","content":"# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\nLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\nwithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\nEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\nproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\nshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\ncasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\nthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\nwritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\nconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n\nThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\nto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\nin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\nof it.\n\n## What to do\n\nValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\nunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\nschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\nproviders.\n\nCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\nnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\nresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n\nA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\nso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\ngeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\nit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\nthere is no opt-out flag.\n\nUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n      count is `0`\n- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n      exactly once\n- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n      **not** contain the offending value\n- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n      that the thrown message names the tool\n- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n      round\n- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n      pinned by a test\n- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n      containing that field's value\n- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n      `devDependencies`, and\n      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n      merely present\n- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n      returned to the model rather than thrown\n- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n      properties pass through unmodified\n- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n      bump is MINOR\n- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n\n## Tech notes\n\nToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\nWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\nthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n\nDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\nclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\ndirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\nthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\nevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\nvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\nowns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\nauthorization model); retry, timeout\nand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\nwritten against this contract later.\n\n","numLines":126,"startLine":1,"totalLines":126}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_810b3b31d2814fc997523919","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_77a2a208222648458d0f282b","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Definition of Done\n2\t\n3\t> `ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n4\t> without ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n5\t> Every tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n6\t> providers, but nothing validates what comes back from the model.\n7\t\n8\t**Strategy: `direct`** — `ToolRegistry.register`, `ToolRegistry.execute` and `createChatOrchestrator` already expose the exact entry points this ticket changes, so the acceptance tests call them directly today and fail because schema compilation and input validation are absent.\n9\t\n10\t## Done when these pass\n11\t\n12\t- [ ] **rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor** — `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against `inputSchema`, returns a `ToolResponse` naming the missing required property, and does not invoke the executor.\n13\t  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n14\t\n15\t- [ ] **rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs** — `ToolRegistry.execute` rejects a property with the wrong JSON Schema type without calling the executor, names the failing field path and expected type in `result`, and omits the offending property value from both `result` and emitted log lines.\n16\t  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n17\t\n18\t- [ ] **throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled** — `ToolRegistry.register` compiles a static `ToolDefinition`'s `inputSchema` once at registration time and throws synchronously naming the tool when the schema is invalid.\n19\t  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n20\t\n21\t- [ ] **returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round** — `createChatOrchestrator` feeds a schema-rejected tool call's `ToolResponse.result` back to the provider as a `tool_result` without invoking the executor on the invalid call, allowing the model to retry with valid arguments and complete the turn.\n22\t  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n23\t\n24\t## Facets\n25\t\n26\t- [ ] Add `ajv` to `dependencies` in `package.json` and `package-lock.json` (`strict: false`, `validateFormats: false`, no coercion, no defaults, no injected `additionalProperties: false`) and bump the package version `MINOR`.\n27\t- [ ] Compile static `ToolDefinition.inputSchema` at `ToolRegistry.register` time (throwing an error naming the tool on invalid schemas) and cache compiled validators for function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` schemas by schema object reference (`WeakMap`).\n28\t- [ ] Validate `input` in `ToolRegistry.execute` before invoking the executor, returning a `ToolResponse` (`{result}`) that names failing field paths and expected types/required fields without echoing or logging offending values, and add the one-line doc comment pointing at `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`.\n29\t- [ ] Add unit tests in `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` and `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts`, link the remaining statements in `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, and update `example/weather-tool.ts`, `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md`, `.specify/spec.md`, `adrs/ADR-002-response-validation.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md`.\n30\t\n31\t## Out of scope\n32\t\n33\t- Output validation and domain sanity checks (covered by ADR-002), MCP tool-server discovery and proxying (#42), per-tool authorization or `beforeToolCall` hooks (#40), retry/timeout/rate-limiting around tool execution, and concrete tool implementations.\n34\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8d3e1359-359f-4923-a237-478972687d15","uuid":"ed7b83bb-5d33-4049-b9d9-805c6395eb15","timestamp":"2026-11-05T18:59:42.353Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/6656eaf44017/workspace/target/.lore/dod.md","content":"# Definition of Done\n\n> `ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n> without ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n> Every tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n> providers, but nothing validates what comes back from the model.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — `ToolRegistry.register`, `ToolRegistry.execute` and `createChatOrchestrator` already expose the exact entry points this ticket changes, so the acceptance tests call them directly today and fail because schema compilation and input validation are absent.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor** — `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against `inputSchema`, returns a `ToolResponse` naming the missing required property, and does not invoke the executor.\n  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs** — `ToolRegistry.execute` rejects a property with the wrong JSON Schema type without calling the executor, names the failing field path and expected type in `result`, and omits the offending property value from both `result` and emitted log lines.\n  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled** — `ToolRegistry.register` compiles a static `ToolDefinition`'s `inputSchema` once at registration time and throws synchronously naming the tool when the schema is invalid.\n  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round** — `createChatOrchestrator` feeds a schema-rejected tool call's `ToolResponse.result` back to the provider as a `tool_result` without invoking the executor on the invalid call, allowing the model to retry with valid arguments and complete the turn.\n  `src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] Add `ajv` to `dependencies` in `package.json` and `package-lock.json` (`strict: false`, `validateFormats: false`, no coercion, no defaults, no injected `additionalProperties: false`) and bump the package version `MINOR`.\n- [ ] Compile static `ToolDefinition.inputSchema` at `ToolRegistry.register` time (throwing an error naming the tool on invalid schemas) and cache compiled validators for function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` schemas by schema object reference (`WeakMap`).\n- [ ] Validate `input` in `ToolRegistry.execute` before invoking the executor, returning a `ToolResponse` (`{result}`) that names failing field paths and expected types/required fields without echoing or logging offending values, and add the one-line doc comment pointing at `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`.\n- [ ] Add unit tests in `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` and `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts`, link the remaining statements in `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, and update `example/weather-tool.ts`, `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md`, `.specify/spec.md`, `adrs/ADR-002-response-validation.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md`.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Output validation and domain sanity checks (covered by ADR-002), MCP tool-server discovery and proxying (#42), per-tool authorization or `beforeToolCall` hooks (#40), retry/timeout/rate-limiting around tool execution, and concrete tool implementations.\n","numLines":34,"startLine":1,"totalLines":34}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_77a2a208222648458d0f282b","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/6656eaf44017/home-1/.claude/skills/tdd-loop\n\nThis is the authoritative copy of Lore's TDD contract for agent runs. It is checked\ninto the Lore repo at `apps/mcp-server/agent-skills/skills/tdd-loop/` and served to\npods by the gateway's `/skills` registry.\n\nYou run the three phases yourself, in order, in one pass. Do not skip a phase and do\nnot run two facets in one round.\n\n## The three laws\n\n1. Do not write production code unless it is to make a failing test pass.\n2. Do not write more of a unit test than is sufficient to fail — a compile or import\n   failure counts.\n3. Do not write more production code than is sufficient to make the currently failing\n   test pass.\n\n## Phase 1 — RED\n\nDetect the toolchain from the project (`package.json`, `go.mod`, `pyproject.toml`,\n`Cargo.toml`, `pom.xml`, `Gemfile`, `mix.exs`, `Makefile`, CI config) and mirror the\nconventions already in the repo's tests — framework, directory, file suffix, assertion\nstyle. Assume nothing about the language.\n\n**The one-facet rule.** Name the single smallest behaviour that moves you forward. If\nthe behaviour you were handed would force more than one new facet, write the test for\nthe smallest facet only and report the leftovers rather than growing the round.\n\nWrite **exactly one** test. Run it. Confirm it fails **for the reason you expect** —\nthe behaviour is absent, not the import path typo'd or a fixture missing. A test you\ndid not run is not a red bar, it is a guess.\n\nIf a behaviour would not change the code under test at all, say so and write nothing.\nA test that forces no change is not worth writing.\n\n### No mocks, no stubs\n\nDesign every test to exercise pure logic with real values — value in, value out, not\ninteractions. A facet that seems to *require* a double is a design signal: the real\nproblem is an unmanaged side effect, not the absence of a mock. Pick a facet that is\ntestable without doubles, and note that the production code should push the I/O to the\nedges so the pure core is directly testable.\n\n- Stubbed lookup → pure function on a value. `discountFor(user)` taking a real `User`,\n  not `discountFor(userId)` fetching one behind a `UserRepository` stub.\n- Interaction mock → assert on a returned value. Have the core return the intent and\n  assert `notificationsFor(order)`; sending happens at the edge.\n- Clock/RNG stub → inject the value. `isExpired(deadline, now)`, both plain values.\n\nA true collaborator boundary that must eventually be pinned down is a separate\n**contract test**, never an ad-hoc mock sprinkled into a unit test.\n\n### Test naming\n\nThe principle is language-agnostic; the surface form follows the language.\n\n- Short, clear, scannable. The name carries the expected behaviour **and** the tested\n  data — never the implementation.\n- No \"should\".\n- Apply the *ask-why* check: a name that does not answer why the test exists is a\n  structural name, not an intent-revealing one.\n- Include the context only when the enclosing suite does not already establish it.\n\nGood: `returns 0 for an empty list`, `returns I for 1`, `throws RangeError for 0`.\nBad: `should return zero`, `test sum`, `works correctly`.\n\n## Phase 2 — GREEN\n\nWrite the fewest lines that turn the bar green. No extra features, no speculative\ngenerality, no refactoring yet.\n\n**Kent Beck's green-bar strategies:**\n\n- *Obvious implementation* — when the real code is trivial and unmistakable, write it.\n- *Fake it* — when it is not, return a constant and let a later test replace constants\n  with variables.\n- *Triangulate* — leave generalization to the NEXT test. Do not guess the general\n  solution now.\n\nRun the tests you are working against — the one you just wrote plus the acceptance\ntests that define done — by naming their files. Never the repository's whole suite:\nCI runs that on every push, and the pod's Bash hook refuses a bare `npm test` or\n`vitest run` with no path. All of what you run must pass.\n\n**Never edit the test to make it pass.** If the test is wrong, say so and stop; that is\na finding, not a green bar.\n\n### Where code goes\n\nNever create code in catch-all `utils` / `misc` / `helpers` / `common` / `shared` /\n`lib` modules. Place and name code by the domain concept it serves — discount logic in\n`pricing/`, not `utils/calc.ts`. When no home exists, create a domain-named module,\nnever a generic bucket.\n\n### Naming\n\nMeaningful but concise — one to three words (`pendingOrders`, `isContainer`,\n`retryCount`). No single letters except trivial loop indices; no `temp`/`data`/`x`; no\nsprawling four-plus-word names. Name by role: `rawInput`, `normalized`.\n\n## Phase 3 — REFACTOR\n\nThe bar is green. Improve the design without changing behaviour, and fit the new code\ninto the surrounding architecture. The suite stays green throughout.\n\n**Duplication is duplicated knowledge, not just duplicated text.** Hunt for and\ncollapse: the same business rule expressed two ways, a constant or validation\nre-derived in two places, parallel structures encoding one concept, a domain concept\nnamed differently across modules, the same idea living in both code and a test.\n\nRefactor **both** production and test code. Test refactoring counts: collapsing\nduplicated tests into one parameterized test, removing now-subsumed tests, extracting\nshared setup, clarifying names. Behaviour coverage must not shrink.\n\n### The Simple Design Dynamo\n\nReuse is a **result** of removing duplication, never a prediction. Never extract because\nsomething \"might\" be reused. For each candidate, in order:\n\n1. Remove the duplication — collapse the copies into one. The reusable shape only\n   becomes visible after the duplication is gone.\n2. Separate what varies from what stays the same.\n3. Extract a template — the algorithm steps with explicit variation points.\n4. Name each operation concretely. **If a step cannot be named, the variation is not\n   understood yet — stop and look.**\n5. Parameterize the details — lift literals into arguments. Generic logic in code,\n   specific values in data passed in.\n\nThis loops: removing duplication creates structures → you name them → better names\nraise cohesion → higher cohesion makes the next, bigger duplication visible.\n\n**Rule of three.** Two copies = notice, and remove the duplication *in place*. Promote\nto a shared module only on the third genuinely similar case. Lifting on the second copy\ncouples unrelated callers and is harder to undo than the duplication was.\n\n### Reveal intent\n\nFor any name you touch, ask \"why does this exist / what is it really?\" — the name must\nanswer that, not describe the mechanism. A function name containing \"and\"\n(`parseAndStore`) is two responsibilities; split it. Improve names iteratively:\nnonsense → structurally accurate → intent-revealing.\n\n### Fewest elements\n\nInline indirection that does not pull its weight — a wrapper that only forwards. Apply\n\"too simple to break\": never add a test for trivial composition of already-trusted\nparts. Reject premature abstraction.\n\n### Refactor discipline — this is where rounds go wrong\n\n- **Never change production code and test code in the same step.** Each step touches\n  only tests or only production code, then run the suite to green before the next.\n  Changing both at once means a green bar no longer proves the refactor was safe.\n- One target per step, always.\n- **If a step turns the suite red, revert that step** rather than chasing the failure.\n- Grep for existing abstractions to reuse before inventing new ones.\n- May move or extract code and create files, but never add behaviour or expand scope\n  beyond the current test.\n\n### Out-of-scope smells\n\nWhile you have the context loaded, note architecture and code smells you spot that are\n**unrelated** to the current round — god objects, leaky abstractions, missing seams,\ndead code. **Do not fix them**; that blows the round's scope. Collect them as\n`future_improvements`: location + smell + one-line suggested fix. Report them; act on\nnone.\n\n## Ending the round\n\nCommit and push per the delivery contract in your task instructions — in a pod, the\nwork dies with the container otherwise. Commit only on green; a red suite is never\npushed.\n\nReport: the facet you closed, the facet you would take next, the post-refactor test\nresult, and the `future_improvements` list.\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8d3e1359-359f-4923-a237-478972687d15","uuid":"81d14660-d705-4ef4-b382-922be6194fbe","timestamp":"2026-11-05T18:59:45.075Z","isSynthetic":true}

=> {\n13\t    setLogger(undefined);\n14\t  });\n15\t\n16\t  it('rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor', async () => {\n17\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n18\t    let executorCalls = 0;\n19\t    registry.register(\n20\t      {\n21\t        name: 'get_weather',\n22\t        description: 'Get weather for a location',\n23\t        inputSchema: {\n24\t          type: 'object',\n25\t          properties: {location: {type: 'string'}},\n26\t          required: ['location'],\n27\t        },\n28\t      },\n29\t      async () => {\n30\t        executorCalls += 1;\n31\t        return 'sunny';\n32\t      }\n33\t    );\n34\t\n35\t    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n36\t\n37\t    expect({\n38\t      executorCalls,\n39\t      mentionsRequiredField: response.result.includes('location') && /required/i.test(response.result),\n40\t    }).toEqual({\n41\t      executorCalls: 0,\n42\t      mentionsRequiredField: true,\n43\t    });\n44\t  });\n45\t\n46\t  it('rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs', async () => {\n47\t    const recordedLogs: string[] = [];\n48\t    const recordLine = (category: string, message: string, fields?: Record<string, unknown>) => {\n49\t      recordedLogs.push(JSON.stringify({category, message, fields}));\n50\t    };\n51\t    const logger: Logger = {\n52\t      debug: recordLine,\n53\t      info: recordLine,\n54\t      warn: recordLine,\n55\t      error: recordLine,\n56\t    };\n57\t    setLogger(logger);\n58\t\n59\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n60\t    let executorCalls = 0;\n61\t    registry.register(\n62\t      {\n63\t        name: 'ssn_lookup',\n64\t        description: 'Look up record by identifier',\n65\t        inputSchema: {\n66\t          type: 'object',\n67\t          properties: {ssn: {type: 'string'}},\n68\t          required: ['ssn'],\n69\t        },\n70\t      },\n71\t      async () => {\n72\t        executorCalls += 1;\n73\t        return 'found';\n74\t      }\n75\t    );\n76\t\n77\t    const response = await registry.execute('ssn_lookup', {ssn: 123456789});\n78\t\n79\t    expect({\n80\t      executorCalls,\n81\t      mentionsField: response.result.includes('ssn'),\n82\t      mentionsExpectedType: response.result.includes('string'),\n83\t      leaksValueInResult: response.result.includes('123456789'),\n84\t      leaksValueInLogs: recordedLogs.some(line => line.includes('123456789')),\n85\t    }).toEqual({\n86\t      executorCalls: 0,\n87\t      mentionsField: true,\n88\t      mentionsExpectedType: true,\n89\t      leaksValueInResult: false,\n90\t      leaksValueInLogs: false,\n91\t    });\n92\t  });\n93\t\n94\t  it('throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled', () => {\n95\t    const registry = new 'unreachable'\n108\t      )\n109\t    ).toThrow(/broken_weather/);\n110\t  });\n111\t\n112\t  it('returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round', async () => {\n113\t    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n114\t    const executorInputs: Array<Record<string, unknown>> = [];\n115\t    registry.register(\n116\t      () => ({\n117\t        name: 'get_weather',\n118\t        description: 'Get weather for a location',\n119\t        inputSchema: {\n120\t          type: 'object',\n121\t          properties: {location: ${String(input.location)}`;\n128\t      }\n129\t    );\n130\t\n131\t    let firstRoundToolResult = '';\n132\t    let round = 0;\n133\t    const provider: AIProvider = {\n134\t      async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n135\t        round += 1;\n136\t        if (round === 1) {\n137\t          yield {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-1', {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'};\n149\t          return;\n150\t        }\n151\t        yield {type: 'text', text: '22C in Paris'};\n152\t        yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n153\t      },\n154\t      async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n155\t        return {} as T;\n156\t      },\n157\t    };\n158\t\n159\t    const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(\n160\t      provider,\n161\t      new PromptBuilder({identity: 'You are a test assistant.'}),\n162\t      registry\n163\t    );\n164\t    const session: ChatSession = {\n165\t orchestrator.processMessage(session);\n171\t\n172\t    expect({\n173\t      reply,\n174\t      executorInputs,\n175\t      firstAttemptRejectedForLocation:\n176\t        firstRoundToolResult.includes('location') && /required/i.test(firstRoundToolResult),\n177\t    }).toEqual({\n178\t      reply: '22C in Paris',\n179\t      executorInputs: [{location: 'Paris'}],\n180\t });\n183\t});\n184\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8d3e1359-359f-4923-a237-478972687d15","uuid":"d05b0478-551a-401f-80e5-dde6cb5da2b7","timestamp":"2026-11-05T18:59:54.196Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/6656eaf44017/workspace/target/src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts","content":"import {ToolRegistry} from './registry.js';\nimport {createChatOrchestrator} from '../chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport {PromptBuilder} from '../../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\nimport {setLogger} from '../../shared/logger.js';\nimport type {Logger} from '../../shared/logger.js';\nimport type {AIProvider, MessageChunk, SendMessageParams, ToolResultMessage} from '../../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../../types/session.js';\n\nconst SENT_AT = '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z';\n\ndescribe('ToolRegistry inputSchema validation acceptance', () => {\n  afterEach(() => {\n    setLogger(undefined);\n  });\n\n  it('rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let executorCalls = 0;\n    registry.register(\n      {\n        name: 'get_weather',\n        description: 'Get weather for a location',\n        inputSchema: {\n          type: 'object',\n          properties: {location: {type: 'string'}},\n          required: ['location'],\n        },\n      },\n      async () => {\n        executorCalls += 1;\n        return 'sunny';\n      }\n    );\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n\n    expect({\n      executorCalls,\n      mentionsRequiredField: response.result.includes('location') && /required/i.test(response.result),\n    }).toEqual({\n      executorCalls: 0,\n      mentionsRequiredField: true,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs', async () => {\n    const recordedLogs: string[] = [];\n    const recordLine = (category: string, message: string, fields?: Record<string, unknown>) => {\n      recordedLogs.push(JSON.stringify({category, message, fields}));\n    };\n    const logger: Logger = {\n      debug: recordLine,\n      info: recordLine,\n      warn: recordLine,\n      error: recordLine,\n    };\n    setLogger(logger);\n\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let executorCalls = 0;\n    registry.register(\n      {\n        name: 'ssn_lookup',\n        description: 'Look up record by identifier',\n        inputSchema: {\n          type: 'object',\n          properties: {ssn: {type: 'string'}},\n          required: ['ssn'],\n        },\n      },\n      async () => {\n        executorCalls += 1;\n        return 'found';\n      }\n    );\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('ssn_lookup', {ssn: 123456789});\n\n    expect({\n      executorCalls,\n      mentionsField: response.result.includes('ssn'),\n      mentionsExpectedType: response.result.includes('string'),\n      leaksValueInResult: response.result.includes('123456789'),\n      leaksValueInLogs: recordedLogs.some(line => line.includes('123456789')),\n    }).toEqual({\n      executorCalls: 0,\n      mentionsField: true,\n      mentionsExpectedType: true,\n      leaksValueInResult: false,\n      leaksValueInLogs: false,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled', () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n\n    expect(() =>\n      registry.register(\n        {\n          name: 'broken_weather',\n          description: 'Has an invalid JSON Schema type',\n          inputSchema: {\n            type: 'object',\n            properties: {location: {type: 'not-a-json-schema-type'}},\n          },\n        },\n        async () => 'unreachable'\n      )\n    ).toThrow(/broken_weather/);\n  });\n\n  it('returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const executorInputs: Array<Record<string, unknown>> = [];\n    registry.register(\n      () => ({\n        name: 'get_weather',\n        description: 'Get weather for a location',\n        inputSchema: {\n          type: 'object',\n          properties: {location: {type: 'string'}},\n          required: ['location'],\n        },\n      }),\n      async input => {\n        executorInputs.push(input);\n        return `22C in ${String(input.location)}`;\n      }\n    );\n\n    let firstRoundToolResult = '';\n    let round = 0;\n    const provider: AIProvider = {\n      async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n        round += 1;\n        if (round === 1) {\n          yield {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-1', name: 'get_weather', input: {}}};\n          yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'};\n          return;\n        }\n        if (round === 2) {\n          const lastMessage = params.messages[params.messages.length - 1] as ToolResultMessage;\n          firstRoundToolResult = lastMessage.content[0]?.content ?? '';\n          yield {\n            type: 'tool_use',\n            toolCall: {id: 'call-2', name: 'get_weather', input: {location: 'Paris'}},\n          };\n          yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'};\n          return;\n        }\n        yield {type: 'text', text: '22C in Paris'};\n        yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n      },\n      async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n        return {} as T;\n      },\n    };\n\n    const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(\n      provider,\n      new PromptBuilder({identity: 'You are a test assistant.'}),\n      registry\n    );\n    const session: ChatSession = {\n      sessionId: 's-retry',\n      userId: 'u1',\n      entries: [{role: 'user', content: 'Weather in Paris?', timestamp: SENT_AT}],\n    };\n\n    const reply = await orchestrator.processMessage(session);\n\n    expect({\n      reply,\n      executorInputs,\n      firstAttemptRejectedForLocation:\n        firstRoundToolResult.includes('location') && /required/i.test(firstRoundToolResult),\n    }).toEqual({\n      reply: '22C in Paris',\n      executorInputs: [{location: 'Paris'}],\n      firstAttemptRejectedForLocation: true,\n    });\n  });\n});\n","numLines":184,"startLine":1,"totalLines":184}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_99d83fa27f624d62ba139a24","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_3caed0abc4d145f0b1456b9d","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Tool Input Validation\n2\t\n3\t| Field  | Value                |\n4\t| ------ | -------------------- |\n5\t| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n6\t| Status | In Progress          |\n7\t\n8\tEvery tool registered in `src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts` declares a JSON Schema `inputSchema` that is forwarded to the configured AI provider, and `ToolRegistry.execute` validates the model-supplied `input` against that schema before calling the tool's executor. When validation fails, `execute` returns a rejection `ToolResponse` describing what was wrong rather than throwing, so the orchestrator feeds the failure back to the model as a `tool_result` within the existing tool-round budget.\n9\t\n10\t## Validation at execute time\n11\t\n12\t- When `input` omits a property listed in `required`, `ToolRegistry.execute` returns a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names the missing property as required and never invokes the executor ([validated by: rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L16)).\n13\t- When `input` supplies a property whose value does not match its declared JSON Schema type, `ToolRegistry.execute` returns a `ToolResponse` naming the failing field path and expected type without invoking the executor, and neither `result` nor the emitted log record contains the offending property value ([validated by: rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L46)).\n14\t- When `input` satisfies `required`, declared property types and `enum`, `ToolRegistry.execute` invokes the executor once and passes the input object through unchanged — including undeclared extra properties — without injecting `additionalProperties: false`, coercing types or applying defaults.\n15\t- When `ToolRegistry.execute` is called with a tool name that has not been registered, it throws `Unknown tool: <name>` because an unregistered tool is a programming or configuration error rather than a recoverable input mistake.\n16\t\n17\t## Schema compilation and caching\n18\t\n19\t- A static `ToolDefinition` has its `inputSchema` compiled once at `register` time, so a schema that is not valid JSON Schema throws synchronously from `register` with an error message naming the offending tool ([validated by: throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L94)).\n20\t- A static tool's `inputSchema` is compiled at `register` time and reused on every `execute` call without re-reading or re-compiling `definition.inputSchema`.\n21\t- A function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` resolves its definition on each `execute` call and validates against the `inputSchema` returned for that call, caching compiled validators by schema object reference (`WeakMap`) so distinct returned schemas each enforce their own rules.\n22\t\n23\t## Orchestrator tool-loop recovery\n24\t\n25\t- Because a schema rejection returns a `ToolResponse` instead of throwing, `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` sends the rejection `result` back to the provider as a `tool_result` message, allowing the model to issue a corrected tool call on the next round and complete the turn within `maxToolRounds` ([validated by: returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L112)).\n26\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8d3e1359-359f-4923-a237-478972687d15","uuid":"20e5b813-92c9-4274-8ea1-e168952696d5","timestamp":"2026-11-05T18:59:54.486Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/6656eaf44017/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md","content":"# Tool Input Validation\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n| Status | In Progress          |\n\nEvery tool registered in `src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts` declares a JSON Schema `inputSchema` that is forwarded to the configured AI provider, and `ToolRegistry.execute` validates the model-supplied `input` against that schema before calling the tool's executor. When validation fails, `execute` returns a rejection `ToolResponse` describing what was wrong rather than throwing, so the orchestrator feeds the failure back to the model as a `tool_result` within the existing tool-round budget.\n\n## Validation at execute time\n\n- When `input` omits a property listed in `required`, `ToolRegistry.execute` returns a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names the missing property as required and never invokes the executor ([validated by: rejects input missing a required property with a ToolResponse and never calls the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L16)).\n- When `input` supplies a property whose value does not match its declared JSON Schema type, `ToolRegistry.execute` returns a `ToolResponse` naming the failing field path and expected type without invoking the executor, and neither `result` nor the emitted log record contains the offending property value ([validated by: rejects a wrong-typed property naming the field and expected type without leaking the offending value to the result or logs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L46)).\n- When `input` satisfies `required`, declared property types and `enum`, `ToolRegistry.execute` invokes the executor once and passes the input object through unchanged — including undeclared extra properties — without injecting `additionalProperties: false`, coercing types or applying defaults.\n- When `ToolRegistry.execute` is called with a tool name that has not been registered, it throws `Unknown tool: <name>` because an unregistered tool is a programming or configuration error rather than a recoverable input mistake.\n\n## Schema compilation and caching\n\n- A static `ToolDefinition` has its `inputSchema` compiled once at `register` time, so a schema that is not valid JSON Schema throws synchronously from `register` with an error message naming the offending tool ([validated by: throws at register time naming the tool when a static inputSchema cannot be compiled](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L94)).\n- A static tool's `inputSchema` is compiled at `register` time and reused on every `execute` call without re-reading or re-compiling `definition.inputSchema`.\n- A function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` resolves its definition on each `execute` call and validates against the `inputSchema` returned for that call, caching compiled validators by schema object reference (`WeakMap`) so distinct returned schemas each enforce their own rules.\n\n## Orchestrator tool-loop recovery\n\n- Because a schema rejection returns a `ToolResponse` instead of throwing, `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` sends the rejection `result` back to the provider as a `tool_result` message, allowing the model to issue a corrected tool call on the next round and complete the turn within `maxToolRounds` ([validated by: returns a schema rejection as a tool_result in the orchestrator loop so the model can correct the call on the next round](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L112)).\n","numLines":26,"startLine":1,"totalLines":26}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_3caed0abc4d145f0b1456b9d","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
